Prompt Notes: Nike Pegasus 43 Product Page
Main prompt (summary)
Build a mobile-first Nike Pegasus 43 product page in Next.js (App Router), React and Tailwind CSS, using the provided stock JSON (Pegasus 43 size and stock list.json, 26 sizes). Section order: product photos, shoe name and price, size selector, technical details, customer reviews. On a 375px phone the product photo is 250px tall and the size grid starts at about 458px, inside a 553px usable viewport (667px reference device).

Size buttons are at least 44px wide and 48px tall, with clear in-stock, low-stock, selected and sold-out states; sold-out sizes stay visible but disabled. Reserve space for images, fonts and the grid to prevent layout shift; include loading and error states, accessible labels and keyboard navigation.

Workflow: plan first (PLAN.md), approval, then implementation.
Design decisions
- Fixed height budget (PLAN.md §3): header 48 + photo 250 + dots 20 + name block 88 + 8 padding + 44 heading = grid at 458px. 95px inside the 553px target and 209px inside the 667px reference.
- Stack: Next.js App Router + TypeScript + Tailwind 3. The stock file is read on the server (lib/stock.ts), validated and sorted by display_order; only the gallery, size selector and accordion are client components.
- Grid: 4 columns, 8px gaps, 48px rows, fixed 384px box (7 rows for 26 sizes). Below 300px wide it switches to 3 columns with a taller fixed box.
- Button content: M 9.5 and W 11 · EU 43. The price is shown once, above the selector.
- States never rely on color alone: low stock = amber dot + "Only N left" status line; selected = black fill + check icon; sold out = gray + strike line + "sold out" in the accessible name. Every state uses the same 2px border, so nothing changes size.
- Accessibility: radiogroup / radio roles, descriptive labels, one Tab stop with Arrow/Home/End navigation that skips sold-out sizes, polite live status line, visible focus rings.
- Layout stability: fixed section heights, explicit image dimensions, system font stack (no font swap), skeletons with identical dimensions, error fallback in the same footprint.
- No invented content: photos are placeholders, technical details say "Details to be provided", reviews show an empty state.
- A client/server split was needed: shared types live in lib/sizes.ts so client components never import Node's fs.
Testing notes
Automated checks (local):
- npm run typecheck: passes.
- npm run build: passes; the prerendered HTML contains 26 size buttons (17 in stock, 5 low stock, 4 sold out) and the price once.
Browser checks (dev server, 375×553 viewport):
- Photo 250px, name block 88px, size section 520px, grid top at 458px, first row ends at 506px.
- 26 buttons, all 48px tall and 79.75px wide; no horizontal scroll.
- Clicking a sold-out size does nothing; clicking M 9 selects it and shows "M 9 selected. Only 3 left."
- ArrowRight from M 9.5 skips sold-out M 10 and lands on M 10.5; exactly one Tab stop in the group.
- Size label text fits on one line in every button, including the longest (W 17.5 · EU 50.5).
- 375×667: grid top 458px, first 3 rows fully visible, no horizontal scroll.
- 320×568: cells 66×48px, no horizontal scroll, grid top 458px. Below 360px the label drops the word "EU" and uses 9px text so it fits (the accessible label keeps it). Below 400px the text is 9px.
- Error state: with data/stock.json temporarily renamed, the page shows "Couldn't load sizes" + a 44px Retry button in the same 520px size section; after restoring the file, Retry loads all 26 sizes. (A first version relied on a React error boundary and left the skeleton on screen forever; fixed by catching the error in the server component.)
Known gaps:
- Screen-reader testing and a Lighthouse CLS measurement were not run.
- Data to double check: M 9, M 9.5 and M 10 are low or sold out while neighbouring sizes are well stocked; launch_date (2026-12-10) is not used.
Stage 3 — Slow 4G Performance Trace Prompt
Objective
Verify that the Pegasus 43 size grid remains stationary during a slow mobile page load, and compare measured results against Diego Salazar's Web Platform Slow 4G Mobile Check Profile and the three values in speed-budget.json. Do not infer passing results from code inspection alone.
Exact prompt for Claude Code / Chrome DevTools MCP
Open the running Pegasus 43 page in Chrome DevTools MCP. Enable mobile emulation at a 375 × 812 CSS px viewport with device pixel ratio 3 and touch/mobile enabled. Use Slow 4G: 150 ms RTT and 1638.4 Kbps throughput, with a 4× CPU slowdown. For real DevTools throttling, align request latency and download/upload throughput with Diego's profile where supported (562.5 ms request latency, 1474.56 Kbps download, 675 Kbps upload).

Start a performance recording before navigation, then load the page and continue until the image, price, font rendering, stock data, and all 26 size buttons have settled. Save the trace artifact and report the tested URL, viewport, network/CPU settings, and trace location.

Inspect [data-testid="main-photo"], [data-testid="price"], [data-testid="size-grid"], [data-testid="size-button"], and [data-testid="size-button-sold-out"]. Measure the top edge of the size grid in CSS pixels from first paint through fully loaded. List every shift that moves it, including the timestamp, before/after top positions, total movement in pixels, responsible late element, and evidence from the trace. Distinguish measured movement from hypotheses.

Specifically investigate late main-image dimensions, late title/price content, font swaps, loading skeleton replacement, and stock-data arrival. For each confirmed cause, explain the smallest code fix that prevents movement: reserve intrinsic image dimensions or aspect ratio; reserve stable title/price block height; use compatible/system fonts or metric-adjusted fallbacks; and keep skeleton, error, and final size-grid containers the same size. Apply only necessary fixes, then repeat the same trace to verify the effect.

Compare results against exactly the three root-level speed-budget.json values: performance_score_min: 90, size_grid_shift_px_max: 0, and size_button_min_px: 44. Use a mobile Lighthouse run configured to Diego's profile to measure the performance score; do not treat a DevTools trace alone as a Lighthouse score. Check the rendered smaller dimension (width or height) of every available, low-stock, and sold-out button; all 26 must meet the 44 CSS px floor.

Report a concise before/after table with measured grid movement, Lighthouse performance score, smallest size-button dimension, fixes made, and pass/fail against each budget. If Chrome DevTools MCP, the target page, or Lighthouse is unavailable, state exactly what could not be measured. Do not invent trace values, CLS measurements, or passing results.

Current verification status (2026-11-19)
- Chrome DevTools MCP was not available in the Claude Code session, so a real Slow 4G trace was not recorded. The size-grid shift and Lighthouse performance score remain unverified.
- A code review found reserved space for the 250px main photo, the 88px title/price block, and the 384px size grid beginning at 458px. These are implementation details, not trace measurements.
- The five required data-testid selectors were added. The built page was reported to contain one main-photo, one price, one size-grid, 22 size-button elements, and four size-button-sold-out elements.
- speed-budget.json was added at the repository root with exactly 90, 0, and 44; npm run typecheck and npm run build passed in the local project.
- Next validation step: run the exact trace prompt above in an environment with Chrome DevTools MCP, inspect any observed shifts, apply targeted fixes if needed, and rerun until measured results meet the budget. Do not claim that the performance budget passes until this is done.
